They still run on every commit. **The set is global, not per version** - a calculator takes its figures as inputs, so the flag means the command passed on the commit the verifier ran from, plus the binding test for the one version whose figures the examples use. **Every G21 method reproduction lands inside the 2% bound**; the widest is the production bonus at 1.4% (SARS's R7 954 is the rates figure for about R40 870, not R40 500) and is named in its docstring. Two G01 examples do NOT reproduce and are strict xfails naming O-43 rather than being left out. |
| D-284 | 24 Sep 2026 | Calculators (P7) | **Every calculator's trace now reproduces its own outputs from its own recorded inputs, and a property proves it for every calculator** - the phase's own "Done when" ("a finalised payslip is reproducible from its trace alone") stated as a Hypothesis property rather than an example. `calculators/tests/test_payroll_properties.py`: UIF never over the ceiling it was handed on either side; SDL levied only on a liable employer and then at exactly the rate; PAYE monotonic in taxable income and in the annual payment, and never over the taxable income, on RANDOM consistent bracket ladders; gross, leave pay and termination never negative; invariant 6 (rounded IS exact rounded, exact at six places) through every Money every calculator returns; PAYE plus UIF never exceeding the gross; and a replay of every trace. Examples: `PAYROLL_PROPERTY_EXAMPLES`, default 40 and derandomised (the calculators' branch gate measures test code, and a random 40 can miss a helper branch); run at 2 000 locally | **Five trace defects found, all fixed in the CODE.** (1) `gross.gross_pay` recorded `days=len(data.days)` and no day at all - a gross figure could be reproduced only from attendance rows a reversed run may delete; each day is now `day_NN`, thirteen pipe-separated facts (`gross.day_as_text`). (2) `termination_payout` did not record `days_per_week`, `hours_per_week` or the s35(4) window's weeks, weeks available and remuneration - a variable earner's payout could not be replayed; `leave_pay` always had them. (3) A severance rule read for an operational dismissal and then zeroed by s41(4) or by no completed year was not in `statutory_rows`, and (4) nor was s40(c)(i)'s ratio when the ledger's accrual won - rows are now recorded when READ, not when they produce a line. (5) UIF's and SDL's `exemption_reason` was recorded only as an OUTPUT; now an input too, still echoed in outputs so stored traces keep their shape. **Two INVARIANTS were wrong, and were corrected rather than the generator narrowed (D-176's rule):** "SDL is zero exactly when the exemption flag is set" fails for a liable employer with nothing leviable, and for one cent at a tiny rate that rounds to nil at six places. **Generators are WEIGHTED, never narrowed**: seven draws in eight valid, one from a range including what the calculator refuses, and a refusal is accepted only as the calculator's own type - an arithmetic error escaping fails the property. `st.one_of(valid x 7, none)` did not do this: Hypothesis leans to the simplest branch, and 86% of termination examples refused until an integer draw replaced it. **"Net never negative" is only half buildable**: net needs every deduction on a payslip, which is chunk 8's assembly; the statutory half is a property now and the rest is recorded there. "Balance equals ledger" is P6's and already a property. |
| D-285 | 24 Sep 2026 | Calculators / Payroll (P7) | **COIDA is an accumulation, capped once per employee per assessment period and never pro-rated, and the period is an INPUT.** `calculators/coida.py::assessment_earnings()` sums the lines whose `is_coida_base` flag is set, floors a net-negative total at nil with a warning, and caps at the `COIDA_ANNUAL_CEILING` row it is handed; exactly at the ceiling is declared in full. `payroll/coida.py` reads FINALISED payslip lines whose pay period's PAYMENT DATE falls in the period, reads the flag off each line's component at accumulation time, resolves the ceiling on the period's first day, and pins the employee's tenant itself | **Golden, and DEL-published** - the only worked example the Department publishes for anything this build computes: GN 2390 of 2024 (GG 50386, 27 March 2024) p5, "A Maximum Earnings is applied annually at the end of the assessment period to the individual employee's annual total earnings, not per month. Full annual maximum earnings will apply irrespective of the number of months the employee was employed ... If an employee has earned a total earnings of R600 000.00 ... the amount should be capped at R568 959.00", and GN 1723 of 2023 in the same terms on R529 264. **The assessment period's dates coincide with the tax year's** - GN 2390 states "(01 March 2023 to 29 February 2024) - Assessment Period" and Notice 3910 of 2026's CF-2A heads its columns "Actual Earnings: 01/03/2025 - 28/02/2026 Provisional Earnings: 01/03/2026 - 28/02/2027" - so the brief's "the assessment year is not the tax year" is true of the INSTRUMENT and not of the dates; nothing reads a `tax_year` row for it. **What is loaded**: `COIDA_ANNUAL_CEILING` R668 000 from 1 March 2026, open-ended. **What is not**: Notice 3910's (b) "Minimum Assessment of R1 621" and (c) "R560 for domestic employers" exist only in that row's notes string - and they are minimum ASSESSMENTS, floors on the premium payable, which the accumulation does not use and P8's return does; loading them is reference data and was not done while the verification pass is open. Nor is the 2025/26 season's R633 168 (the same CF-2A's "Actual Earnings" column), which only a return of pre-March-2026 earnings needs - this product has none. **The flag is read, not restated** (D-89): a test flips a component's flag under the maintenance lock and the figure moves with no code change, which is how an answer to O-06 (3607) or O-15 (severance) will reach it. **Not built**: the CF-2A's monthly columns, the directors/members split, and the assessment itself (earnings x tariff) - all P8's `coida_return_of_earnings`. |
| D-286 | 24 Sep 2026 | Calculators / Payroll (P7) | **The statutory annual bonus is one formula, one cycle table and one line on the termination payout, and a sector without a bonus produces no rows at all.** `calculators/bonus.py::annual_bonus()`: each FULL calendar month of service in the cycle earns one twelfth of the rule's weeks at that month's weekly wage - twelve months is SD1 3(3)(a), fewer is 3(3)(b)(i) ("The number of full calendar months service divided by 12 and multiplied by four point three three three times the employee's weekly wage"), and there is no second path. The cycle is the twelve calendar months ending with the rule's payment month (January-December for both instruments loaded). `payroll/models.py::AnnualBonusCycle` is sheet 02's `annual_bonus_cycle` plus `accrued_amount_exact` (invariant 6, CHECKed against the rounded column as `payslip_line` is) and `accrued_as_at`, under FORCE RLS; `payroll/bonus.py::accrue()` rebuilds each row from the engagement, the remuneration history and the rule set, never incrementally (D-153). `TerminationInput.annual_bonus` prices the leaver's share as a `BONUS_PRO_RATA` line, closing D-224's gap | **An instrument without a bonus REFUSES in the calculator and returns None from `bonus_input()`**, so the accrual writes nothing for a domestic employer - a row of nil would read as a bonus that came to zero, which is a different fact; a test runs twelve month-ends for a household and asserts zero rows. **The mid-year leaver, by hand**: SD1 Area A, R1 497,15 a week (GN R.7083 clause 3(1)), employed since 1 February 2024, leaves 20 June 2026 - January to May are full months, June is not - 5 / 12 x 4,333 x 1 497,15 = R2 702,98 (2 702,979563 exact), asserted in the pure test and again end to end from the rows. **Which wage**: SD1's "the employee's weekly wage" is the wage at payment or termination; BCCCI 4.5(d)'s default prices each month at its prevailing rate, taken on the month's last day. **The BCCCI elections (D-242) are read only for an employee whose rule set is AREA-scoped**, the BCCCI being the only such instrument loaded; a test reads every shipped fixture and fails if a second appears. A casual is `contract_type = casual`. **Weekly wage** comes from a new `employees.remuneration.weekly_wage()` - the D-106 hub recomputed from the captured rate and the monthly factor in force, not back-multiplied from a stored derived rate. **Not built**: the December payment and the `paid`/`pro_rata_paid` transitions (a payroll run's, chunk 8), an EMPLOYER-DEFINED bonus (not statutory; it must be captured, and nothing here assumes one), and months from an earlier engagement in the same cycle (O-26's s84). **The cycle reading is flagged, O-44.** The migration `payroll/0005_annual_bonus_cycle` is NOT applied to the development database by this change. |
| D-287 | 24 Sep 2026 | Leave (P6, reviewed from P7) | **The whole-day overdraw is NOT closed, and should stay until Kobus decides otherwise - it costs the employee no entitlement, only timing, and closing it is a schema change against D-188's settled amendment.** `leave/applications.py:583-596` falls the overdraw to unpaid a whole day at a time, backward from the last day, and since D-188's 17 September amendment the covered-but-unused part stays ON the balance. (2) **The schema cannot hold a split day**: `leave_application_day` is unique per (application, date) with ONE `is_paid` boolean and ONE quantity, and `attendance_day` carries one `leave_day_portion`, so paying 3 of 8 hours means a new paid-quantity column on the day row, a second attendance representation, a split TAKEN posting in `approve()`, and P7 pricing that reads the split - a P6 schema change in a phase declared complete. (3) **On a DAYS basis it is not even well-defined**: balances accrue in fractions (one day per 17 worked), so covering "part of a day" would pay 0,235 of a day, below the half-day minimum increment a salaried basis carries (D-174). **If Kobus wants it closed, the feasible version is HOURS-basis only**: an employee election at submission ("use the 3 hours I hold towards this day"), a `paid_hours` column on `leave_application_day` with a CHECK `0 <= paid_hours <= hours`, `approve()` posting TAKEN for `paid_hours`, and `unpaid_hours` becoming the remainder. One question for Kobus: "When an hourly employee applies for more hours than they hold, should the hours they do hold pay part of the last day, or stay on the balance for later, as now?" |
